: Mr. MS Singhvi, Advocate General with Mr.

Mr. Vivek Tyagi, DGC
HON'BLE MR. JUSTICE SANJEEV PRAKASH SHARMA
Judgment / Order
01/10/2020
(1) These bunch of writ petitions raise common question and,
therefore, have been heard together and are being decided by this
common order.
(2) The brief facts which require to be noticed for adjudication of
the bunch of these writ petitions are that the National Health
Mission Rajasthan through Rajasthan State Health Society invited
applications from eligible candidates for the post of Community
Health Officer (hereinafter referred to as 'CHO') on purely
contractual basis under advertisement dated 31/08/2020 and in
all 6310 posts were advertised for being filled up on contract
basis.

(150 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
(3) As per the advertisement, the minimum educational
qualification required for the post has been mentioned as B.Sc. in
Community Health or Nurse (GNM or B.Sc) or Ayurveda
Practitioner (BAMS) from recognized university and secondly must
be registered in respective Rajasthan Nursing Council/Board of
Indian Medicine Rajasthan in Rajasthan.
(4) The age criteria is from 18 years to 45 years as on
01/01/2021 and monthly honorarium has been fixed as Rs.
25,000/- per month.
(5) All the petitioners who have preferred these writ petitions
have assailed the condition of the advertisement which lays down
the essential educational qualifications and limits them only to the
3 qualifications as above but does not include BHMS (Bachelor in
Homeopathic Medicine and Surgery), BDS. (Bachelor in Dental
Surgery), B.Pharma (Bachelor in Pharmacy) and Bachelor in
Naturopathy and Yogic Sciences.
(6) The petitioners have filed separate petitions wherein the
candidates posses the aforesaid qualifications of BHMS, BDS,
B.Pharma and Bachelor in Naturopathy and Yogic Sciences and
such similar qualifications and it has been submitted that as all
these qualifications are in the nature of general medicine and in
one way or the other relate to health management, the candidates
who posses the said qualifications must also be treated as
equivalent qualification and included in the advertisement and
petitioners be allowed to participate in the selection process for
the post of 'CHO'.
(7) Learned counsel for the petitioners have stated that the
National Health Policy, 2017 was published by the Ministry of
Health, Family Welfare, Government of India wherein the post of

(151 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
Mid Level Health Providers was earmarked as one of the posts
required to be recruited for expansion of primary care from
selective care to comprehensive care, complimentary human
resource strategy and it was observed as under:-
"This can be done through appropriate courses like a
B.Sc. in community health and/or through
competency-based bridge courses and short courses.
These bridge courses could admit graduates from
different clinical and paramedical backgrounds like
AYUSH doctors, B.Sc. Nurses, Pharmacists, GNMs,
etc. and equip them with skills to provide services at
the sub-centre and other peripheral levels. Locale
based selection, a special curriculum of training close
to the place where they live and work, conditional
licensing, enabling legal framework and a positive
practice environment will ensure that this new cadre
is preferentially available where they are needed
most, i.e. in the under-served areas."
(8) Learned counsel for the petitioners further submitted that
the word 'AYUSH' doctors as suggested herein-above, means and
includes all doctors who are practicing in Ayurveda, Yoga, Unani,
Sidhha and Homeopathy.
(9) In view of above, the State is bound by the National Health
Policy issued by the Central Government and as the selections are
being done under the National Health Mission, all the petitioners
who are practicing in the various fields can be considered as
eligible for the post of 'CHO' and the action of the State
Government in excluding them is unjustified.
(10) By certain interim orders, some of the petitioners were
allowed to submit their applications for participating in the
selection process. However, the selection process has not started
as yet apart from inviting the applications.

(152 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
(11) The State Government in the first case namely, Raghvendra
Agarwal and others Vs. State & ors. (SB Civil Writ Petition
No.10342/2020) has filed reply which as prayed for has been
treated as a leading case by this Court.
(12) A preliminary objection has been raised by the State and it is
submitted that the controversy raised in the writ petition is
beyond the scope of the judicial review and has relied on following
judgments:-
"(i) (2020) 4 SCC 86: Mukul Kumar Tyagi v. State of
U.P.
(ii) (2019) 2 SCC 404: Zahoor Ahmad Rather vs.
Sheikh Imtiyaz Ahmed.
(iii) (2009) 1 SCC 546: T. Venkateswarulu v.
Tirumala Tirupathi Devasthanams
(iv) AIR 1965 SC 491: University of Mysore v. C.D.
Govinda Rao
(v) State of Rajasthan and Ors. Vs. Lata Arun
(2002) 6 SCC 252"
(13) Learned Advocate General for the State has stated that the
controversy regarding recruitment to the post of 'CHO' has already
come up before different High Courts and the Hon'ble High Courts
have negated the similar claim and the judgments delivered by
the various High Courts have been placed on record as under:-
(i) Nitish Paul Sharma and others Vs UOI (Himachal
Pradesh High Court (Division Bench) decided on
1.7.2020.
(ii) Mahendra Singh Yadav Vs State of UP
(Allahabad High Court) decided on 19.9.2019
(iii) Abhishek Parmar Vs State of MP (Madhya
Pradesh High Court, Gwalior Bench) decided on
8.11.2019.

(153 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
(iv) Mohan Lal Kumawat and others Vs State of
Madhya Pradesh (Madhya Pradesh High Court, Indore
Bench) decided on 25.11.2019.
(v) Vinod Gunkar Vs State of MP (Madhya Pradesh
High Court, Gwalior Bench) decided on 22.11.2019.
(14) Learned Advocate General on merits has also taken this
Court to the 'AYUSHMAN BHARAT Comprehensive Primary Health
Care through Health and Wellness Centers Operational Guidelines'
as published by National Health Mission and Section 4 of which
relates to Human Resources and sub-section 4.1 of Section 4
relates to Mid Level Health Provider which reads as under:-
"4.1. Mid Level Health Provider
A key addition to the primary health team at the SHC-HWC, would be the Mid-level Health Provider (MLHP)
who would be a Community Health Officer (CHO) - a
BSc. in Community Health or a Nurse (GNM or B.SC)
or an Ayurveda practitioner, trained and certified
through IGNOU/other State Public Health/Medical
Universities for a set of competencies in delivering
public health and primary health care services.
The rationale for introducing this new cadre of health
provider is to:
 Augment the capacity of the Health and
Wellness Centre to offer expanded range of services
closer to community, thus improving access and
coverage with a commensurate reduction in OOPE.
. Improve clinical management, care coordination
and ensure continuity of care through regular follow
up, dispensing of medicines, early identification of
complications, and undertaking basic diagnostic tests.
. Improve public health activities related to
preventive and promotive health and the
measurement of health outcomes for the population
served by the HWC.

(154 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
This will improve utilization of health services at
primary care level, reduce fragmentation of care, and
work load at secondary and tertiary care facilities.
Districts will be encouraged to find MLHPs from within
their district. However, since not all districts may
have adequate availability of eligible candidates, it
may be appropriate to plan MLHPs as a State cadre
that will adhere to state specific cadre management
rules. The state should make cadre rules and
communicate to districts. States could also explore
the possibility of creating a district cadre."
(15) Thus, it is submitted that the National Health Mission also
has not included the other qualifications apart from the
qualifications as mentioned in the advertisement for the purpose
of appointment of CHO.
(16) Learned Advocate General further submitted that the
question regarding the request being sent from different States in
inclusion of Homeopathy and Unani practitioners for the post of
CHO was examined by the Government of India and vide its letter
dated 17/12/2019, the Government of India has taken a stand
with regard to Homeopathy Practitioners, B.Pharma and Unani
Practitioners and has not accepted the contention of inclusion of
Homeopathy Practitioners, B.Pharma and Unani Practitioners for
the said purpose. However, with regard to Unani practitioners in J
& K and Maharashtra States, the said Unani practitioners have
been allowed keeping in view the relevant conditions in the said
States.
(17) Learned Advocate General submitted that the said condition
does not prevail in Rajasthan and therefore, such a demand could
not be accepted. Be that as it may, learned Advocate General
submitted that it is in exclusive domain of the State employer to

(155 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
decide the qualifications which requires for a participant and no
right can be claimed by the petitioners for being included in the
selection process.
(18) I have considered the submissions.
(19) The Supreme Court in Banarsidas & Ors. Vs. State of UP
& Ors: AIR 1956 (SC) 520 has examined the scope of judicial
review with regard to the requisite qualifications under an
advertisement and has held as under:-
"(4)....In our opinion, it is open to the appointing
authority to lay down the requisite qualifications for
recruitment to Government service and it is open to
the authority to lay down such prerequisite conditions
of appointment as would be conducive to the
maintenance of proper discipline amongst
Government servants. If persons already under
government employment on part-time basis had
shown themselves not to be amenable to proper
discipline in government offices, it was open to
government not to appoint such persons to the
permanent cadre of Lekhpals because such persons
could not be said to be as efficient as those who had
excellent records of service and had shown greater
sense of responsibility to their employers.
Article 16 of the Constitution is an instance of
the application of the general rule of equality laid
down in Art. 14, with special reference to the
opportunity for appointment and employment under
the Government. Like all other employers,
Government are also entitled to pick and choose from
amongst a large number of candidates offering
themselves for employment under the Government."
(20) In the opinion of this Court, a judicial review of an
advertisement issued by the State would be limited to the aspect

(156 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
as to whether the said advertisement issued by the authority is in
accordance with the rules laid down by the concerned State
authority or the appointing authority.
(21) Thus, while examining the advertisement dated 31/08/2020
and the qualifications laid down there under for appointment as
CHO, the guidelines laid down by the National Health Mission
would have to be looked into.
(22) While learned counsel for the petitioners have pointed out
that in the National Health Policy of 2017, it was provided that for
the post of Mid Level Health Provider AYUSH doctors can be
considered, it appears that a departure has been made by the
appointing authority while issuing the Latest Operational
Guidelines under the 'AYUSHMAN BHARAT' of National Health
Mission and as noted above, under Section 4, the qualifications
laid down for CHO have been limited to B.Sc. in Community Health
or Nurse (GNM or B.Sc.) or Ayurveda Practitioner (BAMS) trained
and certification. Thus, the second condition of being duly
registered with the concerned council, the non-inclusion of BHMS
qualification or other qualifications in related health subjects under
sub-section 4 is not under challenge before this Court.
(23) In view of above, this Court while exercising jurisdiction
under Article 226 could not and would not add some new
qualifications for the post of CHO at the askance of the petitioners.
(24) Neither this Court is having the infrastructure nor the
jurisdiction to examine as to what can be the exact qualification
for a particular post.
(25) In Mukul Kumar Tyagi Vs. State of UP & Ors: (2020)4
SCC 86, the Apex Court has observed that the eligibility
qualification is an exclusive domain of the appointing authorities

(157 of 157) [CW-10342/2020]
and self-certification by candidates of equivalence is not
envisaged. It is settled law that this Court would not be the forum
for declaring qualifications as equivalent for the purpose of
appointment.
(26) In Zahoor Ahmad Rather & Ors. Vs. Sheikh Imtiyaz
Ahmad & Ors.: (2019)2 SCC 404, the Apex Court has reiterated
as under:-
"26. The prescription of qualifications for a post is a
matter of recruitment policy. The State as the
employer is entitled to prescribe the qualifications as a
condition of eligibility. It is no part of the role or
function of judicial review to expand upon the ambit of
the prescribed qualifications. Similarly, equivalence of
a qualification is not a matter which can be
determined in exercise of the power of judicial review.
Whether a particular qualification should or should not
be regarded as equivalent is a matter for the State, as
the recruiting authority, to determine."
(27) The other judgments as cited by learned Advocate General
for the State also are on the same lines and it would not be
required to burden this judgment by citing different paras of the
said judgments. Several High Courts have also followed the same
yardsticks (supra).
(28) Keeping in view above, all these writ petitions claiming
equivalence and consideration for participation in the selection
process are found to be devoid of merit and the same are
accordingly dismissed. No costs.
(SANJEEV PRAKASH SHARMA),J
Raghu/
